  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "mommotti-c01",
      "source_id": "mommotti-s1",
      "quote": "Nella tradizione sarda, mommotti, noto anche come bobbotti, mommoti, momotti, rappresenta una figura immaginaria utilizzata per spaventare i bambini.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam tradisi Sardinia, mommotti (juga bobbotti, mommoti, momotti) adalah sosok khayalan untuk menakut-nakuti anak.",
        "en": "In Sardinian tradition the mommotti (also bobbotti, mommoti, momotti) is an imaginary figure used to frighten children."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mommotti-c02",
      "source_id": "mommotti-s1",
      "quote": "Talvolta viene associato all'uomo nero, oppure a un orco cattivo con il compito di portare via i bambini che non si comportano bene.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kadang ia dikaitkan dengan uomo nero atau raksasa jahat yang bertugas membawa pergi anak yang nakal.",
        "en": "It is sometimes linked to the uomo nero or to a wicked ogre whose job is to carry off children who misbehave."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mommotti-c03",
      "source_id": "mommotti-s1",
      "quote": "Viene solitamente descritto come un uomo vestito con un lungo mantello nero con cappuccio che cela gli occhi. Utilizza un nodoso bastone e porta un paio di scarponi neri e malconci.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia biasanya digambarkan sebagai lelaki bermantel hitam panjang bertudung yang menutupi matanya, membawa tongkat berbonggol, dan memakai sepatu bot hitam usang.",
        "en": "It is usually described as a man in a long black hooded cloak hiding his eyes, with a knotty stick and a pair of battered black boots."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mommotti-c04",
      "source_id": "mommotti-s1",
      "quote": "Ha una folta barba e se si riesce a vederlo senza cappuccio il suo sguardo è terrificante. Passa di casa in casa ascoltando se qualche bambino fa i capricci. In alcune zone della Sardegna viene associato al diavolo.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia berjanggut lebat dan tatapannya menakutkan bila terlihat tanpa tudung; ia berkeliling dari rumah ke rumah mendengarkan anak yang rewel, dan di sebagian Sardinia ia dikaitkan dengan iblis.",
        "en": "He has a thick beard and a terrifying stare if seen without his hood; he goes from house to house listening for naughty children, and in parts of Sardinia he is linked with the devil."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mommotti-c05",
      "source_id": "mommotti-s1",
      "quote": "L'origine del nome potrebbe derivare da Mamuthones, una delle spaventose maschere nere tradizionali dell'isola di Sardegna e la cui tradizione ha origine incerta.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya mungkin berasal dari Mamuthones, salah satu topeng hitam tradisional Sardinia yang menakutkan, yang asal-usul tradisinya tidak pasti.",
        "en": "The name may come from the Mamuthones, one of the frightening traditional black masks of Sardinia, whose tradition is of uncertain origin."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mommotti-c06",
      "source_id": "mommotti-s1",
      "quote": "Secondo lo studioso Raffaele Ballore, tali maschere fanno presumere il loro radicamento in epoche e contesti precristiani.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut peneliti Raffaele Ballore, topeng-topeng itu menunjukkan akar pada masa dan konteks pra-Kristen.",
        "en": "According to the scholar Raffaele Ballore, these masks suggest roots in pre-Christian times and contexts."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mommotti-c07",
      "source_id": "mommotti-s2",
      "quote": "si credet chi bènzat dae su capu de zosso casteddàju, in ube sos arabos e sos piratas irrobàbant a s'ispìssu. Si còntat de unu tzertu Mohammed, arabu dae sa pedde nighedda, chi attaccabat sas biddas a de notte.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Sardinia, di Sardinia selatan yang sering dijarah orang Arab dan perompak, nama ini dikaitkan dengan kisah seorang Arab berkulit gelap bernama Mohammed yang menyerang desa pada malam hari.",
        "en": "According to the Sardinian-language article, in southern Sardinia, often raided by Arabs and pirates, the name is linked to a tale of a dark-skinned Arab called Mohammed who attacked villages by night."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mommotti-c08",
      "source_id": "mommotti-s2",
      "quote": "Un'atera orizine, custa borta de su capu de susu, est cussa de sa famìlia Marotta chi in Còssiga, in s'epoca napoliònica, fachìat parìtzos secuèstros politicos.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Asal-usul lain, dari Sardinia utara, adalah keluarga Marotta di Korsika yang pada masa Napoleon melakukan banyak penculikan politik.",
        "en": "Another origin, from northern Sardinia, is the Marotta family of Corsica, which carried out many political kidnappings in the Napoleonic era."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Mullo adalah mayat hidup atau vampir dalam cerita rakyat Roma, arwah orang yang mati tidak wajar yang kembali untuk mencekik dan mengisap darah kerabatnya.",
    "en": "The mullo is an undead or vampire of Roma folklore, the ghost of someone who died unnaturally and returns to strangle relatives and suck their blood.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Mullo, \"orang yang sudah mati\", adalah mayat hidup atau vampir dalam cerita rakyat Roma. Ia tercipta bila seseorang mati mendadak secara tidak wajar atau tanpa upacara pemakaman yang layak. Ia berpakaian putih, berambut sampai ke kaki, dan punya satu keanehan fisik yang berbeda menurut daerah. Ia kembali untuk mencekik dan mengisap darah, biasanya kerabat yang menyebabkan kematiannya atau melalaikan adat pemakaman.",
      "en": "The mullo, 'one who is dead', is an undead or vampire of Roma folklore. It arises when someone dies suddenly and unnaturally or without proper funeral rites. It wears white, has hair down to its feet and one bodily oddity that differs by region. It returns to strangle and drink the blood of people, usually relatives who caused its death or neglected the burial customs.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "mullo-vampire-c01",
        "mullo-vampire-c02",
        "mullo-vampire-c03",
        "mullo-vampire-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Serbia ia bisa tak terlihat atau tampak normal, dan selalu kembali kepada pasangannya. Untuk menyingkirkannya orang menyewa Dhampir; untuk menangkalnya, jarum baja atau besi ditusukkan ke jantung mayat, hawthorn ditaruh di kaus kakinya, atau mayat dipenggal dan dibakar.",
      "en": "According to the Serbian article it can be invisible or look normal, and it always goes back to its partner. To be rid of it people hired a Dhampir; to ward it off, steel or iron needles were driven into the corpse's heart, hawthorn put in its sock, or the body beheaded and burned.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "mullo-vampire-c05",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "mullo-vampire-c01",
      "source_id": "mullo-vampire-s1",
      "quote": "Mullo (Muli: female, Mulo: male) is an undead, revenant, or vampire of Roma folklore. 'Mullo' means 'one who is dead'.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mullo (Muli untuk perempuan, Mulo untuk laki-laki) adalah mayat hidup, revenant, atau vampir dalam cerita rakyat Roma; namanya berarti \"orang yang sudah mati\".",
        "en": "The mullo (Muli for a female, Mulo for a male) is an undead, revenant or vampire of Roma folklore; the name means 'one who is dead'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mullo-vampire-c02",
      "source_id": "mullo-vampire-s1",
      "quote": "A mullo is created when a person dies suddenly of some unnatural cause or the person did not have proper funeral rites.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mullo tercipta bila seseorang mati mendadak karena sebab tidak wajar atau tidak mendapat upacara pemakaman yang layak.",
        "en": "A mullo arises when someone dies suddenly of an unnatural cause or does not receive proper funeral rites."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mullo-vampire-c03",
      "source_id": "mullo-vampire-s1",
      "quote": "A mullo is described as having white clothes, hair that reaches to their feet, and one physical oddity (a trait which varies from geographic region to region). A mullo's existence is in seeking out people it did not like in life and harassing that person.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mullo digambarkan berpakaian putih, berambut sampai ke kaki, dan memiliki satu keanehan fisik yang berbeda menurut daerah; ia mencari dan mengganggu orang yang tidak disukainya semasa hidup.",
        "en": "A mullo is described with white clothes, hair to its feet and one physical oddity that varies by region; it seeks out and harasses people it disliked in life."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mullo-vampire-c04",
      "source_id": "mullo-vampire-s1",
      "quote": "Mullo are believed to return and do malicious things and/or attack by strangling and suck the blood of a person (usually a relative who had caused their death, or hadn't properly observed the burial ceremonies, or who kept the deceased's possessions instead of destroying them as was proper).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mullo dipercaya kembali untuk berbuat jahat atau menyerang dengan mencekik dan mengisap darah, biasanya kerabat yang menyebabkan kematiannya, tidak menjalankan upacara pemakaman dengan benar, atau menyimpan barang milik almarhum yang semestinya dimusnahkan.",
        "en": "Mullos are believed to return to do harm or attack by strangling and sucking blood, usually from a relative who caused their death, neglected the burial rites, or kept the dead person's belongings instead of destroying them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mullo-vampire-c05",
      "source_id": "mullo-vampire-s1",
      "quote": "To get rid of a vampire people would hire a Dhampir (the son of a vampire and his widow) to detect the vampire.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Untuk menyingkirkan vampir, orang menyewa Dhampir, putra vampir dan jandanya, untuk mendeteksinya.",
        "en": "To get rid of a vampire, people hired a Dhampir, the son of a vampire and his widow, to detect it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mullo-vampire-c06",
      "source_id": "mullo-vampire-s1",
      "quote": "To ward off vampires, Romani people drove steel or iron needles into a corpse's heart and placed bits of steel in the mouth, over the eyes, ears and between the fingers at the time of burial.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Untuk menangkal vampir, orang Roma menusukkan jarum baja atau besi ke jantung mayat dan menaruh potongan baja di mulut, mata, telinga, dan sela jari saat pemakaman.",
        "en": "To ward off vampires, Romani people drove steel or iron needles into a corpse's heart and put bits of steel in the mouth, over the eyes and ears and between the fingers at burial."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mullo-vampire-c07",
      "source_id": "mullo-vampire-s1",
      "quote": "They also placed hawthorn in the corpse's sock or drove a hawthorn stake through the legs. Further measures included driving stakes into the grave, pouring boiling water over it, decapitating the corpse, or burning it.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka juga menaruh hawthorn di kaus kaki mayat atau menancapkan pasak hawthorn menembus kakinya; cara lain adalah menancapkan pasak ke kubur, menyiramnya dengan air mendidih, memenggal mayat, atau membakarnya.",
        "en": "They also put hawthorn in the corpse's sock or drove a hawthorn stake through the legs; other measures were staking the grave, pouring boiling water on it, beheading the corpse or burning it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mullo-vampire-c08",
      "source_id": "mullo-vampire-s2",
      "quote": "Верује се да може бити невидљив, изгледати потпуно нормално, недостајати му прсти или имати животињске делове тела. У Индији се описује са жутом или пламеном косом, док у Шведској поседује способност промене облика.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Serbia menyebut mullo dipercaya bisa tak terlihat, tampak normal, tak berjari, atau berbagian tubuh hewan; di India ia digambarkan berambut kuning atau menyala, di Swedia ia dapat berubah wujud.",
        "en": "The Serbian article says the mullo is believed able to be invisible, look wholly normal, lack fingers or have animal body parts; in India it is described with yellow or flaming hair, and in Sweden it can change shape."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mullo-vampire-c09",
      "source_id": "mullo-vampire-s2",
      "quote": "Једна од често спомињаних особина мулоа је неутољива сексуална жеља, због чега се, према веровању, увек враћа свом партнеру.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu ciri yang sering disebut adalah hasrat seksual yang tak terpuaskan, sehingga ia dipercaya selalu kembali kepada pasangannya.",
        "en": "One often-mentioned trait is an insatiable sexual desire, which is why it is believed always to return to its partner."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Nappateuctli adalah salah satu Tlaloque, dewa hujan Mexica, yang menumbuhkan alang-alang dan menurut Sahagún menciptakan seni membuat tikar.",
    "en": "Nappateuctli is one of the Tlaloque, the Mexica rain gods, who makes reeds grow and, according to Sahagún, invented the art of mat-making.",

Menurut Bernardino de Sahagún ia menciptakan seni membuat tikar, sehingga para pembuat tikar memujanya.",
      "en": "Nappateuctli, 'four times lord' in Nahuatl, is one of the Tlaloque, the rain and spring gods who helped Tlaloc in Mexica mythology, sharing out rain and making reeds grow. According to Bernardino de Sahagún he invented mat-making, so mat makers worshipped him.",

    {

      "en": "He was shown with a black-painted body, holding a water-plant shield and a staff of paper flowers. At his feast a slave dressed to look like the god was sacrificed, after sprinkling everyone with water from a green bowl.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "nappateuctli-c01",
      "source_id": "nappateuctli-s1",
      "quote": "Nappateuctli (del náhuatl: nappateuctli ‘cuatro veces señor’‘nappa, cuatro veces; teuctli, señor’) en la mitología mexica es uno los Tlaloque, dioses de la lluvia y de las fuentes fluviales, por lo que este hacer brotar los juncos y los rosales.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nappateuctli (bahasa Nahuatl, \"empat kali tuan\") dalam mitologi Mexica adalah salah satu Tlaloque, dewa hujan dan sumber air sungai, sehingga ia menumbuhkan alang-alang dan mawar.",
        "en": "Nappateuctli (Nahuatl, 'four times lord') in Mexica mythology is one of the Tlaloque, gods of rain and river springs, and so makes reeds and roses sprout."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nappateuctli-c02",
      "source_id": "nappateuctli-s1",
      "quote": "Según Bernardino de Sahagún en el capítulo XX de su Historia general de las cosas de Nueva España que este (Nappateuctli) es el que inventó el arte de hacer esteras y por eso lo adoran por dios los de este oficio que hacen esteras, que llaman petates.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Bernardino de Sahagún dalam bab XX Historia general de las cosas de Nueva España, Nappateuctli menciptakan seni membuat tikar, sehingga para pembuat tikar (petate) memujanya sebagai dewa.",
        "en": "According to Bernardino de Sahagún in chapter XX of his Historia general de las cosas de Nueva España, Nappateuctli invented the art of mat-making, so mat makers (of petates) worship him as their god."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nappateuctli-c03",
      "source_id": "nappateuctli-s1",
      "quote": "Se le sacrificaban víctimas humanas y era considerado como una especie de Júpiter Pluvius.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Korban manusia dipersembahkan kepadanya, dan ia dianggap semacam Júpiter Pluvius.",
        "en": "Human victims were sacrificed to him, and he was regarded as a kind of Júpiter Pluvius."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nappateuctli-c04",
      "source_id": "nappateuctli-s1",
      "quote": "tiene en la mano izquierda una rodela a manera de ninfa, que es una hierba de agua, ancha como un plato grande y en la mano derecha un báculo florido; las flores son de papel.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia memegang perisai bundar berbentuk tumbuhan air selebar piring besar di tangan kiri dan tongkat berbunga kertas di tangan kanan.",
        "en": "He holds in his left hand a round shield shaped like a water plant, wide as a large plate, and in his right a flowering staff with paper flowers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nappateuctli-c05",
      "source_id": "nappateuctli-s2",
      "quote": "в ацтекській міфології, помічник Тлалока; один із чотирьох тлалоке, відповідальних за розподіл дощу на землі.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Ukraina menyebutnya pembantu Tlaloc, salah satu dari empat tlaloque yang bertugas membagikan hujan ke bumi.",
        "en": "The Ukrainian article calls him a helper of Tlaloc, one of the four tlaloque responsible for distributing rain on earth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nappateuctli-c06",
      "source_id": "nappateuctli-s2",
      "quote": "Його зображали з усім тілом пофарбованими в чорний колір, його обличчя було вкрите грудками шавлі, на потилиці паперовий головний убір.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia digambarkan dengan seluruh tubuh dicat hitam, wajah ditutupi gumpalan biji sage, dan hiasan kepala kertas di tengkuk.",
        "en": "He was depicted with his whole body painted black, his face covered with clumps of sage, and a paper headdress at the back of his head."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nappateuctli-c07",
      "source_id": "nappateuctli-s2",
      "quote": "На свято Наппатеуктлі люди приносили раба йому в жертву, вдягаючи його в прикраси цього бога, імітуючи зовнішній його вигляд. У день, коли він мав померти, йому в руку клали зелену чашу, повну води, і він кропив усіх цією водою.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada perayaannya, orang mengorbankan seorang budak yang didandani dengan perhiasan dewa itu agar menyerupainya; pada hari kematiannya budak itu diberi cawan hijau berisi air untuk memercikkan semua orang.",
        "en": "At his feast people sacrificed a slave dressed in the god's ornaments to look like him; on the day he was to die, he was given a green bowl full of water and sprinkled everyone with it."
